#4246. Marathon launch player numbers are about half those of the Server Slam, at least on PC

Marathon hasn't quite been able to attract the same volume of players the recent Server Slam did.

After a couple of delays, a plagiarism lawsuit, and at least one internal reboot, Bungie’s next big game has officially launched. Marathon, the extraction shooter from the studio behind Destiny, has managed to find a decent, if a little small of an audience.

The game’s player numbers on Steam are being compared to its recent free, open weekend, and not in a favourable manner.

Prior to Marathon’s launch, Bungie held a multi-day Server Slam, which offered anyone interested in playing the game a chance to try out almost all of its classes, two of its maps, as well as many of its gameplay and progression systems.

The open test was largely well-received, and players actually showed up for it. It peaked at a very respectable 143,621 concurrent players on Steam, which put it within striking distance of the peak concurrent of its chief competitor, Arc Raiders - at least when you compare both of their pre-launch Server Slam events.

In Arc Raiders’ case, those figures were far outpaced at the game’s actual launch, and the Embark shooter continues to command a very active player base to this day. A paid product garnering far more players than the same product’s own free sampler is never not a good sign, so many hoped something similar would happen with Marathon.

Marathon has now officially launched, and it doesn’t look like it managed to pull an Arc Raiders - at least so far. At its peak, the Bungie shooter attracted 88,337 concurrent players on Steam (via SteamDB), which is a little over half of its Server Slam’s 143,621 peak concurrent.

Numbers have, of course, dropped since then, which is normal, as players log off for the night. This is obviously not the end of the road for Marathon. Friday, and the weekend, may well push numbers closer to the Server Slam heights.

Indeed, word of mouth is quite positive, with the game currently sitting at a Very Positive user review rating on Steam, based on over 4,600 reviews. As has been proven time and time again in recent years, nothing catapults a game like players emphatically advocating for it to their friends, so it could simply be that mainstream players are cautious.

It will become clear where things settle over the weekend.

#4245. Nearly 20% of Switzerland’s glacier ice has vanished in just five years

Switzerland's glaciers lost more than 5% of their total ice volume in 2026, marking another devastating year for the country's rapidly shrinking glaciers. Although the melting did not quite match the record losses of 2022, glaciers thinned by an average of 2.5 to 4 meters. In some areas, the lower sections of glaciers lost as much as 10 meters of ice.

Several of Switzerland's major glaciers, including the Rhône, Aletsch, and Clariden glaciers, experienced their most severe melting on record in 2026. The losses were even more dramatic for some smaller glaciers, which disappeared entirely. Among them were Bella Tola in the canton of Valais and Griessfirn in the canton of Glarus, both of which have now vanished permanently.

The scale of the decline is particularly striking over the past five years. During that relatively short period, Switzerland has lost nearly 20% of its total glacier ice volume, illustrating just how quickly the country's mountain landscapes are being transformed.

These findings come from the annual report of the Swiss Academy of Sciences (SCNAT), which draws on long-term glacier observations collected by the Glacier Monitoring Network, known as GLAMOS. The monitoring program is based at ETH Zurich and the universities of Fribourg and Zurich in Switzerland.

Extreme Summer Heat and Low Snowfall Accelerate Ice Loss

A combination of unusually little snowfall and prolonged summer heat contributed to the extensive melting. The winter of 2025 to 2026 ranked among Switzerland's ten least snowy winters since records began, leaving glaciers with less snow to protect them from warmer temperatures.

Conditions became even more unfavorable between May and September 2026, when persistent heat and dry weather accelerated ice loss, even at the highest elevations.

During the summer, the elevation at which temperatures reached freezing climbed above 4,000 meters on 76 days. That was more than twice the usual number of days and established a new Swiss record. By September, snow had disappeared even at elevations of 3,500 meters, exposing glacier ice that would normally remain protected beneath a layer of snow.

Melting Glaciers Provide Water, but the Supply Is Shrinking

The enormous amount of melting also temporarily helped ease Switzerland's summer water shortages. Between July and September, the country's glaciers released approximately 2.2 trillion liters of water, an amount exceeding four times the drinking water consumed by Swiss households in an entire year.

However, this additional water comes from a resource that is steadily disappearing. As Switzerland's glaciers continue to shrink irreversibly, the amount of meltwater they can provide will also decline, reducing a source of water that currently helps offset shortages during the warmer months.

Materials provided by ETH Zurich. Note: Content may be edited for style and length.

#4244. Roco Kingdom preview - "The monster taming RPG I've long been waiting for"

When you think about magic academies, you almost always assume it has something to do with a skinny boy in round glasses getting whisked away by a friendly bearded giant to a land of witchcraft and wizardry. And when you think about creature collecting, it's always about a boy in a red cap stuffing capture balls in his backpack and setting off from his small town to discover the world.

It's incredibly refreshing, then, to have both of these elements combined in Roco Kingdom, where you enrol in a magic academy not to learn spells with a magic wand but to catch the aforementioned creatures in the wild - red cap and round glasses optional.

Catching 'em all, finally I'm not going to beat around the bush - I honestly think that Roco Kingdom is the creature-collecting game I've been waiting for. Too many entries in the genre have tried to emulate that Pokémon feel but somehow still manage to either "modernise" it in the wrong way or end up copying the popular Nintendo property. But what MoreFun Studios has done here is take the monster-taming experience and improve it in more ways than one, and I absolutely love the result.

For one thing, the creatures themselves - called Jinies - don't look like distant cousins of Pikachu and the gang. And for another, despite the "Pokeballs" still being, well, balls, they're magical in nature, which is perfectly in sync with the magical theme of the world of Carosia.

And because it's an open world, you get to venture out from your magic school and really explore every nook and cranny of the fantasy land laid out beautifully before you. Carosia's colourful setting has effectively reignited that sense of excitement inside me that disappointing games in the genre have long since snuffed out. It's been a long time since I really felt the desire to explore the world around me, and the equally colourful Jinies in the wild only add to that exhilaration.

Because yes, it really is all about the creature-collecting here - not the crafting, not the farming, and certainly not the tedious resource gathering. While there's the option to build your own home and decorate and farm and all that, that's exactly it - optional. Building your home is a side-quest, and farming crops equally so. Roco Kingdom, from the get-go, wants to tell you that it's not a survival crafting or cosy life sim - it's a monster-taming RPG first and foremost, and at its purest and most beautiful form.

And that - to someone like me who's never been a fan of life sims and all the tedious grinding and crafting that come with them - is a humongous sigh of relief. Jini (not) in a bottle As for the Jinies themselves, they're really just living their best lives out in the wild, each one doing their own thing. You spot some of them goofing around with each other or travelling in packs, and whenever you approach, they can either run away, attack you, or just think you're totally "meh". I love how natural this living, breathing world feels - almost like the Jinies' lives don't revolve around you. Shocking!

You can try to aim your adorable star-themed wand at them to stun them before you approach, as bumping into them triggers turn-based combat. If they're stunned, they'll be Dizzy and will miss the first turn, giving you the upper hand in battle. You might want to sneak up behind them too to further boost your advantage, which is handy if you ever want to weaken them before catching.

If you defeat them instead, you'll gain experience points for your Jinies, which you'll need to level them up and eventually evolve them. I didn't feel like levelling up was a chore either, because, like the devs during the media brief I attended said, they wanted to make sure that players can focus on the fun of collecting and levelling up their Jinies. Because if they added all the grindiness that usually comes with evolving Jinies, then what would be the point, right?

And I appreciate that decision so very much, because it seems like the team really wants us to enjoy the creature-collecting aspect of the RPG the most. And right now, I think it's working - I spent a good chunk of my entire weekend just exploring Carosia and catching all sorts of Jinies I spotted in the wild. And they're incredibly adorable too, because you can pet them and feed them to give them the TLC they deserve. It's a not-so-small world, after all

Outside of the very, very strategic combat (thanks to all the distinct skills they have), Jinies also help you out when you're adventuring, as you'll often stumble upon unknown points of interaction in the wild. This usually involves a chest or a shiny spot that can only be "activated" when you chuck a specific Jini element at it, or you can mount your Jini to swim, ride through open fields or glide through the skies from one cliff to the next.

Sometimes, mounting a Jini means they actually mount you by either propping themselves up on your shoulder or having you carry them instead, which is just the most wholesome thing ever, and something other monster-taming adventures out there should definitely have more of.

You'll also encounter a bunch of secret spots and intriguing side-quests while you're roaming around the world, like this Adventurous Duckiji I happened to chance upon. It was teetering on the edge of a cliff while staring intently at something, and right next to it was a bunch of wooden planks nailed together with a drawing of a Duckiji haphazardly put together. It also had a "Satisfied Treasure Hider" description on it - oh, the horrors this Duckiji must have seen!

If you ever get tired of exploring the land, you can rest in the Manaboughs you've activated, which act as save points that let you recover your HP and Vitality. You can also pass the time here, as various Jinies will only pop up during the day/night. There are Nightmare versions of Jinies and Alpha Jinies to take on, Jini Blossom challenges to defeat, and Outdoor Duels to accept - really, there's no limit to what you can do out there, so it's hard to believe it's all free to play. The best things in life are free Of course, this is where concerns about monetisation creep in, but the devs have assured us that microtransactions will only be for cosmetics, and everything can be traded and obtained through gameplay - which is incredibly generous, to be honest. And for the quality of what I've played and the huge amount of content on offer, I'd even say it might be worth making this a premium game instead. Again, the devs have said that there's really no point in paywalling the Jinies because it would defeat the whole purpose of the RPG.

I suppose it's pretty obvious by now that Roco Kingdom has indeed won my heart from this early access build alone. The whole experience feels wonderfully polished, the voice acting is stellar, and the soundtrack is just superb. I wouldn't mind having the music tracks (that you can collect too!) playing in the background on loop while I get some work done during the day.

There's a bit of a multiplayer element here too, by the way, since you can be invited into another player's world by holding hands with them and adventuring together. I admit I wasn't able to experience this much because I was far too engrossed in doing my own thing for hours on end, but it's a nice option for social butterflies looking to play with their besties online.

No grinding, no RNG progression, and mobile-optimised with full cross-platform play - suffice it to say that Roco Kingdom is one to watch when it launches in early to mid-2027. We were told that the global version will have its first two seasons before it syncs up with the Chinese version, but if you're keen on getting first dibs, the Closed Beta Test - adorably dubbed the "Quack Quack Test" - starts today!

#4243. Cyberpunk 2077's new dual wielding has already been modded to include shotguns and rifles, provided you're down to get "janky and wildly overpowered"

It was only ever going to be a matter of time, but I'm still surprised just how quickly Cyberpunk 2077 modders have taken pistol dual wielding mechanic the RPG's Edgerunners 2 update has just added in, and opened it up to include any gun Night City has to offer. Yep, twinning shotguns, assault rifles, sniper rifles, or any combo of those is now possible. Though, you'll have to make peace with the fact enemies will struggle to match your firepower.

A number of different modders instantly decided to take on the task of extending the tag which governs Cyberpunk's new dual wielding perk to include everything with a trigger. There's Dual Wield Anything by long-time Cyberpunk modder E3roKK, Dual Wield Anything and Everything for 2.5 by Jollywankers, and Expanded Dual Wielding by JericoHellden. All three have been released within the last 24 hours.

JericoHellden's mod also includes the option to control each gun separately, rather than CD Projekt Red's default of automatically alternating between the two when firing. If you're only interested in twinning pistols, but do want the ability to independently fire each gun, another new mod - Independent Pistol Controls (Dual Wielding) by Dederara - might be for you.

Here are clips showing Jollywankers' (directly below) and Dederara's (the further down of the two) works in action:

It's worth noting that, in line with the reason CD Projekt Red gave for limiting it to pistols in their official update, dual wielding other guns without any measures in place to make enemies tougher does blunt the challenge of fighting your way through Night City. JericoHellden declared it a "janky and wildly overpowered" experience, and E3roKK made clear that anyone expecting animations not to get a bit clippy or awkward at times is barking up the wrong tree.

If you're hoping to dual wield katanas, unfortunately it looks like melee weapons will take a bit more time to figure out properly, with some of these mods including them only as a 'try it but don't expect things to work yet' afterthought to the ranged main course. Makes sense, given the official integration is designed for lead slingers rather than slappy cybersticks.

The latest version of Cyber Engine Tweaks, the framework mod needed for any Cybeprunk mod setup that's not limited to the likes of light visual tweaks, is a requirement for all for of the mods mentioned here. Redscript is also needed for Expanded Dual Wielding.

#4242. COD: Modern Warfare 4 DMZ players are spawning in the middle of a road and being flattened before they can do anything

Infinity Ward opened up early access to Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4's DMZ extraction mode overnight, meaning anyone who has pre-ordered a copy can now play the game's campaign as well as DMZ. And while early reactions to DMZ are fairly positive, there's one issue in particular, which a number of players have experienced, that has the capability of ending a round as soon as it begins.

The issue is to do with where DMZ spawns players. As an extraction shooter, DMZ's Hajin map offers multiple random spawn locations, and unlike traditional multiplayer modes, DMZ spawns are often on the edges of the map - far away enough from any action to allow players a chance to strategise and pick a target destination.

But unfortunately, one of the spawn-points Infinity Ward picked happens to be placed in the middle of a road, meaning that if you're unlucky, you might spawn as an enemy patrol is driving on it, flattening you and ending your round.

This nearly happened to Reddit user grillimperator, who was given a small window of time to jump out of the way. Reddit user Deep-Operation-3552, however, wasn't so lucky, immediately falling to the ground as soon as they got control of their character after the infil cutscene. As others noted in the replies, the timing of the 'Welcome to the DMZ' voice alert playing just as they die makes it all the more absurd. Judging by those examples, at least, there doesn't appear to be any form of spawn protection.

DMZ offers certain options to get back up after being downed, but if you don't have the necessary equipment, there's nothing you can do. Still, that wouldn't have mattered in the above instances, because the players were run over twice.

Given the persistent nature of DMZ, dying also means losing everything you entered the match with, which could be infuriating if that was the round you decided to pack your good gear.

Thankfully, the bug is one of several issues Infinity Ward fixed in a new DMZ patch. The patch notes specifically called out the problem: "Fixed some instances where enemy AI could run over players while infilling."

Modern Warfare 4's final mode, traditional multiplayer, unlocks at the game's full launch this Friday, 23rd October on PC, PS5, Xbox Series X/S and Switch 2. So far, players appear to be happy with the game's campaign, making it of the most well-received COD games on Steam. One player even managed to finish the campaign on its hardest, single-life mode YOLO.

#4241. Four New Features Coming to Your iPhone in iOS 27.2 (and One That Will Be Missing)

If you're like most people, you might still be getting used to iOS 27 on your iPhone. Perhaps you haven't even installed it yet, or maybe you're confused about why iOS 27.0.1 exists. (The short answer: The iPhone 18 Pro is a bit glitchy.) But there's a new iPhone update on the horizon that adds even more new features and changes to the mix: iOS 27.2. While this update isn't nearly as big of an overhaul as iOS 27 itself, it's still impactful—even if Apple already removed its best new feature. Here's what's new.

The update is currently in testing, so if you want to try it out early, you can enroll your iPhone in Apple's beta and check these features out today. Just know that all betas come with some level of risk, so if you decide to go down this path, create a proper backup of all important data before installing the beta.

Call Context is an excellent use of Apple Intelligence. When you're on a phone call, Apple Intelligence checks if the phone number you're calling is related to a business that you have relevant information for elsewhere on your device. If so, it fetches that information for you and displays it on the call screen. For example, if you're talking with your airline's customer service line, Call Context can proactively display your flight number based on the itinerary it finds in your email.

With iOS 27.2, Call Context now includes anniversary alerts. If you're on a phone call with someone you have a saved anniversary for, that alert will appear on-screen. That way, you can get a small reminder if you call a friend on their birthday, but you happened to forget the occasion. (Thank you, technology, for saving some of us from our thoughtlessness, even if you are enabling bad habits.)

As part of a previous announcement of accessibility features, Apple revealed it was adding a new API to FaceTime that supports adding a human interpreter to video calls. This feature did not make it to iOS 27, but it appears to be rolling out in Apple's next update, making video calls more accessible going forward.

If you have an Apple TV, you likely know you can set up profiles, so individual users can personalize the experience when watching shows and movies. As MacRumors highlights, these profiles are now coming to the Apple TV app in iOS and iPadOS 27.2. That way, multiple users can personalize the Apple TV experience on a single device: If your family shares an iPad, for example, each user can switch to their Apple TV profile when it's their turn to watch. Now, if we could only get system-level profiles too, Apple.

At launch, Siri AI is only available in English. But with iOS 27.2, Apple is expanding that list. Once you update to the latest version, you'll be able to use Siri AI in French, Japanese, Korean, Portuguese, and Spanish.

Apple isn't thrilled with how some of its apps look in iOS 27, apparently. MacRumors also spotted that in the Music app, Apple removed some of the red accents and turned certain buttons from red to black—including the Play and Shuffle buttons, and the icons in the Library sections. In subsequent betas, Apple introduced a subtle new Apple Music icon, with a black music note against a white background, and changed the "active tab" color from red to blue.

In the Clock app, "AM" and "PM" are now smaller than the time when setting alarms. In Podcasts, "Up Next" is now "New Episodes," perhaps adding a touch more clarity. Finally, Apple changed "App Access" in Siri to "Excluded Apps," and now allows you to exclude multiple apps from Siri at once.

With the first iOS 27.2 betas, Apple totally overhauled the Health app. Unfortunately, it doesn't seem destined for release just yet. With iOS 27.2 beta 4, Apple removed the new version of the Health app, reverting it to the one you'll find in iOS 27. As such, when I first published this article, there were five new features coming to iOS 27.2. Now, there are four.

When Apple does roll out the new Health app, fitness users will likely welcome many of these new features. With the new Health app, you'll be able to see your Fitness rings within the Health app, rather than the Fitness app; there's a new "Longevity" tab that gives you insights into key aspects of your long-term health, including mental wellbeing, nutrition, daily movement, heart health, metabolic health, sleep, and hearing health. There are also new assessments you can take to analyze your current overall health and where you can improve, including a new "Health Age" feature. You can even allow your iPhone's camera to watch you exercise in order to rate your fitness in categories like flexibility, balance, VO2max, and strength.

This is by far the Health app's biggest revamp in years, and I'm looking forward to exploring the new features when Apple eventually releases it, hopefully in iOS 27.3. For more on what's new with this upgraded Health app, check out Lifehacker senior health editor Beth Skwarecki's guide here. (Beth is particularly fond of the new step count that appears at the top of both the Health and Fitness apps.)

#4240. Nioh 3 patch finally makes summons useful in boss fights, brings free armour for everyone

There's a new Nioh 3 patch out in the wild, and it's one that any player who regularly summons in boss fights will really appreciate.

Team Ninja continues to work on Nioh 3. The developer has been releasing regular updates for the action RPG since its launch in early February. Earlier patches have addressed major bugs like healing item use inconsistencies, and more have arrived since to tackle various balance and loot-related issues.

Today’s patch, version 1.04, is a major one, coming in at well over 1GB. There’s plenty here worth getting excited about, so let’s dig in.

To begin with, this is the patch that adds the promised free armour sets for both Ninja, and Samurai. When Nioh 3 reached one million sales, Team Ninja said it would gift every player the Thunderous set in celebration, and the set’s two variants are now available in-game.

The more interesting side of the patch is, unsurprisingly, its change log. There appears to be a focus on online co-op and AI summons in this one, with various tweaks to the make the process more rewarding, and, in some cases, worthwhile.

The amount of Glory obtained when you complete a mission in multiplayer has been increased, regardless of which player was the host. The other major change mainly affects AI Benevolent Grave summons, making it so bosses will no longer target a single player for almost the entirety of the fight.

While summoning AI helpers has always been useful in Nioh 3, they didn’t always help in boss fights, specifically because the boss tended to focus on the player who summoned them, leaving the AI to run around eating AoE hits before dying.

Patch 1.04 makes a couple of welcome gameplay tweaks to the feel of combat. Ninjutsu, and Onmyo Magic will now hit their targets more consistently in situations where there’s a difference in elevation between the player and the enemy.

In the same vein, you’ll now be able to attack (quick/strong) right after jumping, which removes the pause that previously buffered the attack instead. This could mean even more air combos that don’t specifically require Martial Arts.

You can look forward to a couple of quality of life tweaks in this patch, too, such as the change that allows the game to automatically send any Sudama Soul Cores dropped by merchants to your item box. This happens from the second one onwards, which means you won’t have to pick half a dozen each time you go to sell a batch of items.

Speaking of the item box, the limit of forging materials has been increased for some of them. If you’ve been running Battle Scroll missions and Expeditions for gear, you’ll be happy to know that you can now exit the session from the main menu directly.

There’s plenty more to patch 1.04, including fixes and balance tweaks for several weapons, such as Samurai dual swords, odachi, and more for a few Ninja weapons, too. The patch also brings several bug fixes to the game.

You can read the full set of patch notes on Steam.

"Indie studios produced 20 of the 21 extra breakout launches in the 2026 cohort," reads one industry analysis.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

You'll never play every game. In fact, most people won't play most games—plenty of games release on Steam to approximately 0 user reviews, and last year, more than half of the 19,000 games that came out had fewer than 10. But that doesn't mean indies and anything else outside of the biggest blockbusters are all sent out to die. So far in 2026, all-original indies have been making waves.

That insight comes courtesy of Sensor Tower (via GamesIndustry.biz), which recently published an industry analysis report sharing some trends among the year's breakout hits. For reference, a breakout is defined in the study as a game which sold at least 500,000 units in its first 30 days. While ultimately based on estimates as not all publishers release sales numbers, the report is full of interesting tidbits.

The most noteworthy findings in the report are thus: there were 76 breakouts between September 2025 and August 2026, up from the previous year's 55. Indie breakouts shot up from 13 to 33, making up 43% of the pack, and 35 of the breakouts were non-sequels—around 53%. 27 of those were made by indie studios. Nine of the indie breakouts were friendslop hits like Meccha Chameleon and RV There Yet?, while 18 were debuts in other genres, such as Escape From Duckov and Megabonk.

Triple-A blockbusters have not really budged, according to the report. Last year they had 27 breakouts, this year they had 28 (notably, due to the methodology used, some huge games are excluded from this report: no free-to-play games like Marvel Rivals are included, and Call of Duty isn't accounted for.) But this does suggest a strong showing for cheap, often low-spec indies with novel ideas and multiplayer, and if you're longing for something new amidst all the proximity voice chat, you'll be happy to hear that both non-sequels and non-friendslop are making up a sizable portion of the year's hits.

#4237. EA Is Closing Down Legendary Studio BioWare Amid More Job Cuts

Electronic Arts plans to eliminate roughly 400 positions in an upcoming round of job cuts, per a new report from Bloomberg, and will also be closing the doors on its long-time role-playing game maker BioWare.

Per Bloomberg's Jason Schreier, these cuts represent about three percent of EA's total staff, and roughly 50 at BioWare. A letter sent to staff today from EA's COO David Tinson and Chief Studios Officer Cam Weber said that they've taken a "hard look at our business" and will cut projects, including the next Mass Effect.

"As we focus on these larger opportunities, we've had a small team at BioWare working on early-development for Mass Effect," the letter reads. "We've made the decision not to move forward with this development, which means we’re closing the studio and our Edmonton workspace. BioWare is a legendary studio that helped define the role-playing genre, and we do not take this decision lightly."

Sports, shooters (like Apex Legends), and Star Wars (including the next Jedi title) will continue apace at Electronic Arts. The publisher also has a new Iron Man game in the works, and plans are in the works for more Battlefield, as well as a new collaboration between Respawn and DICE. The staff letter also suggests a planned Sims game will be put on ice in favor of another venture, and that while Skate development will continue, EA will downsize the team working on it.

EA was fully acquired earlier this year by a number of investor groups, including Silver Lake, Affinity Partners, and the Public Investment Fund of Saudi Arabia, taking the publicly traded company private. In its announcement at the time, EA chairman and CEO Andrew Wilson said, "We're entering this next chapter from a position of strength with partners who share our vision and ambition. Together, we'll invest boldly, accelerate innovation, and build the next generation of games and experiences for the hundreds of millions of players and fans who inspire us every day."

A statement from BioWare posted to social media shared the closure as well, highlighting the list of games it has brought to life: Baldur's Gate, Neverwinter Nights, Star Wars: Knights of the Old Republic, Dragon Age, Mass Effect, and Anthem. The team's focus now, per the statement, is on supporting one another through this transition.

"What generations of BioWare built together is something to celebrate and remember," the statement reads. "These games became so much more because you played them, debated your choices, fell in love with the characters, and made these worlds your own. From all of us at BioWare, thank you for being the most meaningful part of our story."

BioWare has been known throughout its history for exactly that: an undoubtedly legendary run of role-playing games where choice, characters, and moments matter. From the early era of D&D-driven adventures to leading the pack in console RPG development with KOTOR, Mass Effect, and Dragon Age, few Western RPG studios boast the legacy BioWare had.

Still, BioWare often sailed on troubled waters. The underwhelming releases of Mass Effect: Andromeda and Anthem tarnished the studio's pristine image in the eyes of some fans. Dragon Age: The Veilguard, the studio's most recent—and final—game was generally better received by critics, but spent a decade in development turmoil. Internal strife over the making of these massive RPGs was the subject of frequent reporting. The term "BioWare magic," referring to how the studio's games would finally come together in the twilight hours of development, became a sticking point as the years went on.

There is no denying, though, how BioWare has played an active hand in molding the role-playing genre over the years. Even a recitation of past works doesn't feel accurate to the impact; the influence of Mass Effect and Dragon Age alone is something we still see ripples of today. Even as other studios have taken up the torch and put their own spin on companion-driven, choices-matter stories, there is still an innate sense of "BioWare" that's hard to remove from those interactions and moments.

Layoffs have become distressingly commonplace, as cuts to legendary teams are announced at a never-ending pace. But to break news writing decorum, I had always hoped, throughout years of working in this industry, that I wouldn't ever have to eulogize a studio like BioWare. A place that shaped my own dive into video games, and I'm sure did the same for many, many others. A roof under which developers worked long hours to craft stories we'd carry with us for years. I'm thankful for that, and hope those affected find somewhere new that's rewarding of their talents.

#4236. Mediterranean diet may boost tiny proteins that protect the heart and brain

A new study suggests that the Mediterranean diet may protect the heart, brain, and aging cells through a surprising biological mechanism involving tiny proteins produced by mitochondria, the structures responsible for generating most of the energy our cells need.

Researchers at the USC Leonard Davis School of Gerontology discovered that people who more closely followed the Mediterranean diet had higher levels of two mitochondrial microproteins called humanin and SHMOOSE. Both have previously been linked to protection against cardiovascular disease and neurodegeneration, the gradual deterioration of nerve cells that occurs in conditions such as Alzheimer's disease.

The study, led by USC Leonard Davis Instructional Associate Professor of Gerontology Roberto Vicinanza, offers a possible explanation for how everyday food choices influence cellular health and the aging process.

"These microproteins may act as molecular messengers that translate what we eat into how our cells function and age," Vicinanza said. "It's a new biological pathway that helps explain why the Mediterranean diet is so powerful."

How the Mediterranean Diet May Protect Aging Cells

The Mediterranean diet emphasizes foods such as olive oil, fish, beans, lentils, fruits, and vegetables. It has consistently been associated with a lower risk of heart disease, type 2 diabetes, and cognitive decline. However, scientists are still working to understand exactly how this eating pattern produces its wide-ranging health benefits.

To investigate, the researchers examined blood samples from older adults who followed the Mediterranean diet to different degrees. Those whose eating habits most closely matched the diet had significantly higher concentrations of humanin and SHMOOSE.

They also showed lower indicators of oxidative stress, a process in which harmful molecules called reactive oxygen species overwhelm the body's protective defenses. Over time, oxidative stress can damage proteins, DNA, and other cellular components, contributing to aging and chronic disease.

Interestingly, the researchers found that certain foods were associated with different microproteins. Higher consumption of olive oil, fish, and legumes was linked to increased humanin levels. Meanwhile, olive oil consumption and eating fewer refined carbohydrates were associated with higher levels of SHMOOSE.

Refined carbohydrates include foods made with heavily processed grains and sugars, such as white bread, pastries, and many sugary snacks. These foods generally contain less fiber and fewer naturally occurring nutrients than their minimally processed counterparts.

"These findings suggest that specific components of the Mediterranean diet may directly influence mitochondrial biology," said USC Leonard Davis School Dean and USC Distinguished Professor Pinchas Cohen, the study's senior author. "Humanin and SHMOOSE could serve as biomarkers for adherence to the Mediterranean diet and have clinical significance."

Biomarkers are measurable biological indicators that can provide information about a person's health or physiological state. In this case, the researchers suggest that humanin and SHMOOSE could eventually help scientists understand how closely someone's diet aligns with healthy eating patterns and how their cells respond.

Tiny Mitochondrial Proteins With Powerful Effects

The findings extend a scientific effort spanning more than 20 years to investigate small proteins produced by mitochondria. Cohen has played a leading role in discovering and studying these molecules, which are increasingly recognized as important participants in cellular health.

Mitochondria are often described as the powerhouses of cells because they produce much of the energy required for essential biological activities. But their role extends beyond energy production. They also help regulate metabolism, cellular stress responses, and processes associated with aging.

Unlike most proteins, which are produced using instructions stored in the DNA inside the cell's nucleus, mitochondrial microproteins originate from genetic instructions within mitochondria themselves.

These instructions are found in small segments of mitochondrial DNA known as open reading frames. Some of these regions were once considered nonfunctional, but researchers have discovered that they can encode biologically active molecules.

One of the best understood is humanin, first identified by Cohen and fellow researchers in 2003. Subsequent studies have associated humanin with improved insulin sensitivity, better cardiovascular health, increased longevity, and protection of cognitive abilities.

Insulin sensitivity refers to how effectively the body's cells respond to insulin, the hormone that helps regulate blood sugar. Maintaining healthy insulin sensitivity is important for metabolic health and reducing the risk of type 2 diabetes.

Another intriguing microprotein is SHMOOSE (Small Human Mitochondrial ORF Over SErine tRNA), which was identified more recently by Cohen's research team.

SHMOOSE has attracted particular interest because of its possible role in protecting the brain. Scientists have found that a genetic variant of SHMOOSE is associated with an increased risk of Alzheimer's disease. The normal version, meanwhile, appears to help protect neurons from damage involving amyloid, a protein that can accumulate abnormally in the brains of people with Alzheimer's.

"These peptides are emerging as key regulators of aging biology," Cohen said. "They connect mitochondrial function to diseases like Alzheimer's and heart disease and now, potentially, to nutrition."

A Hidden Mechanism for Protecting the Heart

The scientists also identified a potentially important connection between humanin and an enzyme called Nox2, which helps generate reactive oxygen species.

Although these molecules have useful roles in normal cell signaling and immune defense, excessive production can contribute to oxidative stress and damage blood vessels and other tissues.

The researchers observed that people with higher humanin levels tended to have lower Nox2 activity. This relationship raises the possibility that humanin helps limit damaging oxidative processes, potentially offering an additional layer of cardiovascular protection.

Based on these findings, the researchers propose that the Mediterranean diet could support heart health in two complementary ways. The diet may help reduce oxidative stress directly while also supporting the production of mitochondrial microproteins that influence pathways involved in cellular damage.

However, the observed associations do not establish that the diet directly increases these proteins or that the proteins themselves prevent cardiovascular disease.

"This could represent a new cardioprotective mechanism of the Mediterranean diet," Vicinanza said.

Bringing the Mediterranean Diet to a Global Audience

Vicinanza's interest in the Mediterranean diet extends beyond investigating its effects inside the body. He has also worked to promote this traditional eating pattern internationally as a way to support human health, cultural heritage, and environmental sustainability.

As part of those efforts, he collaborated with the Municipality of Pollica in Italy, which is recognized as a UNESCO Mediterranean Diet emblematic community.

That collaboration helped support the establishment of the International Day of the Mediterranean Diet through the United Nations.

Observed every year on November 16, the international event is intended to draw attention to the Mediterranean diet's health benefits as well as its cultural traditions and environmental significance.

For Vicinanza, the new findings reinforce the relationship between traditional food practices and modern discoveries about how the body functions.

"We're connecting centuries-old dietary traditions with cutting-edge molecular biology," Vicinanza said. "It supports the idea that healthy eating patterns with little to no ultra-processed foods reflect how humans have eaten over long periods and may create conditions to which mitochondria -- ancient cellular organelles -- are likely adapted."

Could Tiny Proteins Help Unlock Healthier Aging?

Although the findings are promising, the researchers emphasize that the study was relatively small and observational. That means it identified relationships between dietary habits and biological measurements but could not determine whether eating a Mediterranean diet directly caused the differences in microprotein levels.

Other factors, including participants' overall health and lifestyle, could also influence the results.

Nevertheless, the discovery opens an intriguing possibility for the emerging field of precision nutrition, which seeks to develop dietary recommendations based on an individual's biological characteristics and needs.

If future research confirms that dietary changes can reliably influence humanin, SHMOOSE, and related microproteins, these molecules could potentially become useful tools for evaluating nutritional interventions and developing more personalized strategies for healthy aging.

The next step is to determine whether adopting a Mediterranean-style diet actually increases these proteins and whether those changes lead to measurable improvements in cardiovascular health, brain function, or disease risk.

"Our goal is to move from observing associations to understanding causality," Vicinanza said. "If we can harness these pathways, we may be able to design nutritional strategies that promote healthy aging at the molecular level."

The work was funded by the USC Daryl and Irwin Simon Nutrition for Alzheimer's Disease Prevention Research Fund (to Vicinanza), the Hanson-Thorell Family Research Award (to Vicinanza), National Institutes of Health grant P30AG094848 (to Cohen) and PRIN 2022 grant 000031_23_PP_PIGNATELLI_PRIN_2022-B53D23021240006 (to Pignatelli).

Materials provided by University of Southern California. Note: Content may be edited for style and length.

OLEDs are known for having the best picture quality TVs can offer, if you're okay with the trade-offs. One of the biggest cons of OLEDs is their price; if you've never owned an OLED because they've cost too much, Samsung made an OLED that's within reach: the Samsung S90H Series TV. Right now, the 65-inch Samsung S90H Series TV is $1,497.99 (originally $1,897.99) after a 21% discount, reaching its lowest price ever according to price tracking tools. This TV is also considered one of the best OLED TVs for most people.

What makes the Samsung S90H TV stand out is its picture quality, because it's comparable to far more expensive OLEDs. Some other stand-out features are its excellent response time for gaming—9.33 milliseconds, according to CNET's review—and its anti-reflective filter, which makes it a great fit for bright environments.

If you've been wanting to upgrade to an OLED but haven't been able to justify the price, the S90H is an incredibly worthwhile option.

#4234. "What if Larian had shut down after Divine Divinity?": Star Trek Online exec scorns publishers who close teams like BioWare in favour of "AAA veteran" start-ups and AI "magic beans"

It's like "setting fire to millions of dollars of property and equipment"

Breaking up established game development teams in favour of "AAA veteran" start-ups and "magic bean" technologies is like "setting fire to millions of dollars of property and equipment", in the words of Star Trek Online executive producer Thomas Marrone. He thinks it's "wildly irresponsible and self-sabotaging", and that videogame company shareholders should be absolutely furious about it.

Marrone's comments come in response to EA closing BioWare yesterday, but he takes aim at closures and layoffs across the industry. He notes that many of the most successful teams owe their accomplishments to lessons learned from screw-ups, and suggests that if big game publishers just want "number to go up", they should get out of the game-making trade and pour all their cash into investment funds.

"One of the most insightful comments about game development came from a former boss," Marrone wrote on Bluesky this morning. "He said 'Making a game is like starting a band. You can’t throw money at a new studio with random AAA veterans and expect it to crank out a hit.' A good game needs a vision. A good game needs a good team to build it. A good team needs chemistry to work well together. Team chemistry needs years of collaboration to gel. There are certainly 'rockstars' and leaders who are anchors for their team, but they need to have that chemistry and trust with everyone around them to be truly effective.

"The AAA philosophy seems to be to hire four random musicians and expect them to become the Beatles overnight," Marrone went on. "When it does happen: well, we have the songs now, we can just hire different musicians to record new songs when we want another album. It’s truly insane and shows a fundamental misunderstanding of the collaborative creative process at the heart of making games.

"Most studios that generate an overnight hit are actually on the 3rd or 4th title," he said. "Blizzard, Larian, Arrowhead, these places had several games under their belts before their first true blockbuster. Like George RR Martin they had been grinding XP for years before becoming a household name. The current AAA Publisher practice of disbanding studios, even if they ship a game successfully, is so counterintuitive to their own financial future it boggles my mind.

"Like if I were a major shareholder I would be livid," Marrone observed. "This is wanton destruction of these publishers’ most precious assets. It’s like literally setting fire to millions of dollars of property and equipment. It’s wildly irresponsible and self-sabotaging. If big game publishers just want number to go up, fire everyone and just invest it all in mutual funds or something. If you don’t want to actually make a product, and you don’t want to build a business around making a product, just liquidate your assets and stop pretending you want to make anything."

Marrone has been at Cryptic Studios for 16 years, starting out as an associate graphics designer. "I've worked on one game through that whole career, which means I've seen lots of leaders come and go, and lots of versions of the company and the game as it grew, shrunk, and changed," he noted.

Cryptic themselves were founded in 2000, and are also known for Champions Online, City of Heroes and Neverwinter. They've been through a lot of upheaval lately. Cryptic were acquired by (*dread bell tolls*) publishing conglomerate Embracer in 2021, during the latter's early-Covid-era campaign to absorb half the industry. When Embracer belatedly acknowledged that all of their acquisitions had left them up shit creek without a paddle, Cryptic Studios were among the many subsidiaries who had to lay off staff. Then, Embracer sold them to Project Golden Arc, a new company formed by former Star Trek Online publishers Arc and XD Inc, publishers of Torchlight: Infinite and Heartopia.

I'm not sure how many of Cryptic's founding staff are still there, but Marrone makes it sound like they've done a fair job of keeping their teams together. "I have very personal experience working on a team that has gone through all the corpo speak stages 'forming, storming, norming, and performing,'" he wrote. "It can take Y E A R S to get to that final stage. When you do, it's awesome. People are 2, 3, 4x as effective as when they start. But it just. takes. Time."

"It takes time to build a team," Marrone went on. "It takes shared experiences. It takes shared wins AND shared losses. What if Larian had shut down after Divine Divinity? What if Arrowhead had shut down after Magicka? What if Hello Games had shuttered after the initial launch of No Man's Sky? These studios have all had ups and downs but they were given time to grow and learn and their teams got better and better. Not to say those teams were static, but certainly there was a continuity and culture that informed their biggest successes.

"And when you look at the arc of a studio like Bioware, you can begin to see how important it is to keep the teams healthy, how important it is to keep those rockstars and leaders together with the teams that know them," he said. "Mass Effect is a great game. Mass Effect 2 is one of the best games of all time.

"In my current position I understand the pressure of leadership when millions of dollars and hundreds of salaries are at stake," Marrone concluded. "I understand the drive to find a magic bean like NFTs or GenAI or whatever is trending on LinkedIn this week. There's got to be a 'trick.' We've got to find a 'trick.' But." Marrone ends the thread by reiterating the need to "nurture and empower your team".

We've heard plenty of common-sensical commentary like this from other senior devs of late. In April, Bit Reactor boss and former XCOM developer Greg Foertsch spoke to Julian about how he and other Firaxis managers "kept the team together" across development of Sid Meier's Railroads, XCOM: Enemy Unknown, and XCOM 2.

We've also recently seen a number of new "AAA veteran" startups come and go, as investors take fright after an initial buzz of interest during the Covid lockdown period. Former BioWare manager Casey Hudson launched a new studio in 2021 only to shutter the company in 2024. Former Mass Effect and Dragon Age scribe Mac Walters similarly launched a company in 2023, only for publishers NetEase to pull funding a year later. There are going to be a lot more BioWare veterans in the wilds this year, joining many thousands laid off by the likes of Microsoft and Ubisoft.

Replacing gaming tech is not a purrr-fectly fun expense

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Last week, Razer unveiled the latest addition to its long line of cat-ear-having headsets, the Kraken Kitty V3. This new headset is designed to "transition seamlessly from streaming setup to cosplay accessory" thanks to its new 'Cosplay Mode,' which allows you to plug its USB-C cable into a power bank to keep it illuminated for cons and events.As someone who used to cosplay before the word was even mainstream (yes, I'm that old), it's great to see brands like Razer acknowledge some of the most creative and hard-working individuals that have been fueling comic cons for decades, but just be careful when it comes to taking gaming tech to conventions. The $99 (via Razer) gaming headset looks neat, but any tech worn at a busy show floor is going to be a target for anything from damage to theft. I'd feel far more comfortable if you settled for using it to make videos with your friends at home than putting accessories that will be pricey to replace in harm's way.

The Razer Kraken Kitty V3 is currently available through the Razer online store for $99, but it might take a moment before it appears at other US and UK retailers like the rest of the Kraken Kitty line-up.

When I've had cosplays that called for headsets or headphones in the past, I went down the cheap route. Hatsune Miku's Magnet outfit requires a pair of cans with butterfly wing attachments, while mine were fake and built up from foam pieces. I didn't want to lug around real headphones or stick Glow EL wire around tech I actually used that couldn't be removed later. When it came to putting together a Futaba cosplay from Persona 5, I did dabble in a real pair of cans for many years, but grabbed the cheapest, less than £10 pair of wireless black headphones I could find. Futaba, being the nerd she is, likely would not have settled for a cheap headset, and in reality I could see her preferring a Razer headset like the Barracuda V2 Pro instead, but utilizing real and expensive accessories to replace them just wasn't on my mind. By that stage, I was more than familiar with what conventions were like since cosplaying from 2010, and running the risk of damaging or losing something I used for gaming or music every day wasn't going to be an option. Cosplay is expensive enough without factoring in replacing a gaming headset.

That's why, while Razer states that users can "keep the RGB lighting illuminated at conventions, cosplay gatherings, and community events" on its official blog, I'd recommend omitting the 'conventions' part. At $99, the Kraken Kitty V3 is one of the cheapest in the Kitty lineup (especially the Kraken Kitty V3 Pro that costs $179.99 / £179.99), but replacing even $100 when cosplay is pricey enough is not going to be fun.Of course, there's every likelihood nothing will happen to your accessories, and I'm not your mom, so you can entirely do as you please. Just as someone more than familiar with the con circuit and headsets, I don't want to run the risk of your $100 headset, which isn't typically designed to withstand the climate of an over-sold and packed convention, ending up needing to be replaced. That's before I even get into the fact that it weighs 315g, and when adding heavy wigs into the picture, you don't really want that stuck on your head for multiple hours at a time.

If you do want the headset for gaming, streaming, and frolicking around in cosplay at small gatherings and meet-ups with your friends, there's plenty to like here. The pair is equipped with Razer's signature 40mm TriForce audio drivers, which sound great, and they feature a HyperClear cardioid microphone too. They're also Chroma-powered, so you can use Razer's Chroma app to sync them up to any other RGB-riddled Razer tech you have laying around.

However, typically, conventions and expensive tech you don't want to replace or have any harm come to them don't mix. No matter how purr-fectly cool and illuminating they look.

#4232. Xbox follows in PlayStation's footsteps, introducing AMD FSR 4.1, targeting better graphics and performance on Series X/S

AMD, the company that designs the chips inside Xbox and PlayStation consoles, made a big announcement this week: it's bringing the latest version of its advanced, AI-driven image upscaling technology, FSR 4.1, to Xbox Series X, Series S, and handhelds ROG Ally and Ally X.

An older version of FSR is already supported on Xbox, but FSR 4.1 is its most advanced release. You may recall FSR 4.1 arrived on Steam Machine and Steam Deck shortly after the former's launch in the summer, and it has proven to be a useful option for players looking for a sharper image.

FSR works by rendering the game at a lower resolution, which is then upscaled to a higher resolution by using machine learning algorithms. This allows supported hardware to deliver better quality visuals without putting extra strain on hardware, or by giving more consistent performance.

While all Xbox platforms stand to benefit from FSR 4.1's arrival, Series S and the more hardware-constrained Ally and Ally X handhelds could see the biggest gains. In that way, Microsoft is extending the life of the Series consoles by allowing its aging hardware to do more.

The timing of this announcement is particularly notable, too, as just a couple of weeks ago, Sony introduced QSSR - a similar machine learning-based upscaling technology - for PlayStation 5. QSSR is the less capable sibling of PSSR, which is exclusive to PS5 Pro.

FSR 4.1's arrival also comes as new reports suggest Xbox's next generation Project Helix consoles could cost upwards of $1,800, which would significantly hinder its adoption rate, considering its price.

"We've worked very closely with AMD to optimise this technology, giving developers another powerful tool for great performance and visual quality for players," said Xbox chief technology officer (CTO) Scott Van Vliet.

"And this is just the beginning. Our teams are continuing to work together across hardware and software, and there's even more ahead with Project Helix, leveraging FSR Diamond to power what comes next."

AMD did not offer a specific date as to when FSR 4.1 will arrive on Xbox Series consoles, but it's going to be interesting to see how the image quality compares to PS5's QSSR.

#4231. The Sims 4 patch makes it so your public lot gatherings won't be interrupted by rogue Sims

The Sims 4 will soon get a new update to resolve a number of issues, some of which have long caused headaches for players. The latest update will arrive March 17 on PC and Mac, followed by PlayStation and Xbox at an unspecified time later.

Outside of the fixes themselves, the patch also makes good on the recent re-commitment to continue improving quality of life for players, so there’s plenty here longtime players will appreciate.

In the latest episode of The Sims 4’s Laundry List blog series, where the developer goes through the most reported problems with the game and how it plans to solve them, we got a rundown of what to expect from next week’s patch.

You can look forward to five major fixes in this one, but arguably the most important is a change to how Sims determine who they speak with on a lot. This is especially relevant for public lots, where Sims would sometimes interrupt ongoing conversations by barging in and starting a conversation with your Sim.

Going forward, Sims will prioritise familiar Sims with whom they already have rapport, and be less likely to latch onto strangers. If you’ve witnessed a date your Sim was on get interrupted because of that, you’ll certainly appreciate the change.

Speaking of budding romance, Prom is coming back to in-game Calendars, and Sims can once again attend. Maxis said this will work with new and existing saves, and that you should also expect Prom goals to be completable once again.

Pets and animals get some love in this patch, too. First, pets and horses will no longer get lost in Premade Households. The fix prevents that from happening in new saves, but if you already had that happen to you in your current save, it’ll still be there.

You may now also assign animals to an Animal Shed after moving your house to a new lot, so you hopefully won’t be confused as to why they’re stuck in your inventory. This fix applies to existing saves as well as new ones.

Lower down the list of urgent fixes is an update that makes it so Sims’ eye blinking will show fully closed eyes for most interactions. The upcoming patch also comes with a host of Sims autonomy fixes, including one that prevents them from excessively flirting with each other.

Catch up on the full list at the link near the top of the page. And, in case you missed it, Maxis recently announced that The Sims 4 will soon get paid mods, something that many in the community are skeptical about.

#4230. Illusion Connect: Re beginner's guide - 7 tips for a good start

As with any level-gated game, Illusion Connect: Re can be disheartening. However, if you follow our beginner's guide, you'll have a much easier start.

Illusion Connect Re drops you straight into a world threatened by monsters. It has real-time battles and a bunch of amazing characters to choose from, but to help you pass all the stages, I wrote this Illusion Connect: Re guide to help you out.

As you're probably aware, some of the stages are considerably harder than others. You will need to make several upgrades (and adjust your team) before challenging them, and with the help of these tips, you will know exactly what to focus on. Don't panic, it all takes time! Before you start spiralling, you should know that it all takes time - you won't be able to clear all the stages right off the bat because you will need to reach specific CP to even stand a chance. That's not too difficult to acquire, though, since you can upgrade your characters' gear and level them up.

You can also star up your characters quite a bit, which will give you more character shards, but that's something I will cover further in this guide.

For now, let's start with the basics and dive into our first Illusion Connect: Re tip.

You probably know that you can level up any character you want - well, the downside is that resources are limited, and I strongly recommend focusing only on your core team instead of levelling everything up.

I know it's tempting, but focusing mainly on your best SSRs (a tank, a DPS, and maybe a secondary DPS or a Healer - depending on what characters you have) is the best course of action.

If you rerolled, you probably have Miyuki or Yuffie, who are great early on. Invest most of your resources to level them up to max, and then focus on your tank. If you happened to pull Anna from the gacha, then you can also level her up. She's a great character to use in the Arena as well, so it's not a waste of resources.

Illusion Connect Re isn’t a passive auto-battler. Yeah, you can turn on the Auto function, but the AI doesn't always work in your favour. Auto will most often deploy a character you don't want, unless you only have 4-5 characters on your team and you're happy with the order.

The main reason I mention the order here is that Energy is an important resource, and if you use it all to deploy the characters in the wrong order, they don't return to your hand (unless they have a skill that specifically does that).

I cannot tell you the right rotation here, because it all depends on what characters you have and what Leader you're using, but as a rule of thumb, you always want to use a tank that has Taunt before your main DPS source (like Miyuki, Rotania, or Yuffie).

As you probably know, you can change your Leader once you meet the requirements to unlock the option. The Sakura Swordsman is a solid option, and even if it's unlocked by default, it can be used all the way into the late game (if you meet the requirements to unlock all of his skills).

As you can see, you will need to clear the Collapsed Dreams stages as much as you need to clear the regular Dream stages.

The story mode is the backbone of your early progression. Every chapter unlocks new features, resources, and ways to make your team stronger. I recommend you try to clear as many Dream stages as you can, and then you switch to Collapsed Dreams.

You can get a lot of Diamonds for clearing the stages with 3 stars, so I suggest you put Auto battle on hold when the stage is too hard, and try to clear it manually.

The Illusion Connect: Re home system looks like pure decoration, but it adds to your long-term progression. Characters have some interactions there, but most importantly, furniture pieces offer passive bonuses. In short, the whole system becomes a steady way of getting resources.

Even if you’re not the type who enjoys decorating, upgrading the home pays off. It's also cheap to do, and whenever you log back into the game, you should clear the rewards from there (it's also needed to complete one of the daily quests).

Illusion Connect: Re has a pretty generous list of gift codes, and they’re absolutely worth using as soon as you start. You will get a lot of currency and summons, and they will be a huge help in the long term. You will even get gear boxes, which are extremely important if you want to progress faster (those extra stats help a lot).

There are plenty of Diamonds to be claimed while you have story stages to clear, but once you finish them all, you will need another source of Diamonds. That's where the daily and weekly quests come into play. Make sure to clear them all regularly - it only takes 20 minutes or less, so it's worth it.

Since we're on the topic of Diamonds, you should save them all for exclusive pick-up banners that have characters that you need on your team. You don't have to do that from the start, though, so you can have fun with the gacha system (until you unlock some SSRs). After you get your hands on a few top-tier SSRs, I recommend saving the Diamonds.

→ Baca artikel asli

#4229. Commander Shepard actors Jennifer Hale & Mark Meer mourn BioWare: 'It was in the hands of ownership that may not have understood what BioWare fully is''

You can say that again.
